Do not present a recommendation\\nas an agreed architecture decision (ADR). Prepare verification contracts (VER)\\nin [Define how the result will be verified](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#define-how-the-result-will-be-verified).\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\nFor a missing domain, use the first command in action 2 and the second only\\nafter reviewing the preview in action 3:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl scaffold-domain REPO --domain DOMAIN --dry-run --json\\nharnessctl scaffold-domain REPO --domain DOMAIN --json\\n```\\n\\nFor each missing artifact, use this **creation sequence** in actions 5\\u20136.\\nReplace `TYPE` with the table value. Replace `ARTIFACT-ID` in the second\\ncommand with the first command's `allocated_id`:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl create-artifact REPO --domain DOMAIN --type TYPE --dry-run --json\\nharnessctl create-artifact REPO --domain DOMAIN --type TYPE --id ARTIFACT-ID --json\\n```\\n\\nThe preview writes nothing and does not reserve an ID. Creation writes one\\nincomplete template and returns its path. Content completion is a separate\\nediting action. The JSON output does not contain the authoring checklist.\\n\\n**Completion:** Each missing definition has a completed draft with an ID and\\npath. Unresolved choices are identified. If no definitions are missing,\\nrecord `None needed` in the transient inventory.\\n\\n**Later use:** These definitions supply the verification contracts (VER) in\\n[Define how the result will be verified](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#define-how-the-result-will-be-verified) and the governing links in work orders (WO) in [Prepare work orders (WO) linking the definitions, scope, planned work, and verification requirements](DRAFT_WORK_ORDERS.md#prepare-work-orders-wo-linking-the-definitions-scope-planned-work-and-verification-requirements). Their acceptance\\nis handled in the authorization process.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"c38865671a81429dd291d9f9bb333deae02a4098d4afcf034f293ee919e990e0\"}, {\"heading\": \"define-how-the-result-will-be-verified\", \"title\": \"Define how the result will be verified\", \"anchor\": \"define-how-the-result-will-be-verified\", \"start_line\": 109, \"end_line\": 165, \"content\": \"### Define how the result will be verified\\n\\n**Inputs:** The selected requirements (REQ), specifications (SPEC), proposed\\namendments, and existing verification contracts (VER).\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** New or updated verification contracts (VER) in\\n  `draft` where coverage is missing. None created or modified where existing\\n  contracts provide unchanged coverage. Accepted contracts remain unchanged.\\n- **Transient working material:** The selection of reused verification\\n  contracts (VER), any amendment proposals prepared through [Propose any needed amendments to existing definitions](AMEND_DEFINITIONS.md#propose-any-needed-amendments-to-existing-definitions), coverage\\n  gaps, and unresolved questions. Retain these under the same conditions\\n  as [Describe the intended outcome](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#describe-the-intended-outcome).\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Read each selected requirement's (REQ) acceptance criteria and linked\\n   specifications (SPEC).\\n2. Compare those criteria with the existing verification contracts (VER).\\n3. Select unchanged contracts that cover the proposed behavior.\\n4. For each coverage gap, create a verification contract (VER) with the\\n   commands below, or use [Propose any needed amendments to existing definitions](AMEND_DEFINITIONS.md#propose-any-needed-amendments-to-existing-definitions) to propose an amendment to an existing one.\\n5. Complete `Requirement-to-evidence matrix`. For each requirement (REQ),\\n   record its ID, method, case or evidence, and pass condition. Use `test`,\\n   `analysis`, `inspection`, or `demonstration` as the method.\\n6. Specify the inputs, platform, evaluator, and command or manual actions for\\n   each check. Derive expected results from the requirement (REQ) and\\n   specification (SPEC), not from candidate output.\\n7. Set `[relations].verifies` to the covered requirement (REQ) IDs.\\n8. Complete `Independence`, `Evidence retention`, and applicable assessment\\n   sections in each new or edited draft.\\n9. Apply `## verification` \\u2192 `### Checklist` in\\n   `docs/engineering/ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md`.\\n10. Retain questions about missing criteria or methods for [Record unresolved decisions (DEC)](RISKS_AND_DECISIONS.md#record-unresolved-decisions-dec).\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\nFor each new verification contract (VER), run the creation sequence from\\n[Draft any missing definitions](DRAFT_DEFINITIONS.md#draft-any-missing-definitions) with these arguments. Inspect the preview before creation; replace\\n`VER-ID` with its `allocated_id`:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl create-artifact REPO --domain DOMAIN --type verification --dry-run --json\\nharnessctl create-artifact REPO --domain DOMAIN --type verification --id VER-ID --json\\n```\\n\\nNo harness command writes the verification method or pass criteria. The human\\nor agent completes that content. No verification record (VREC) is created here.\\n\\n**Completion:** Every selected requirement (REQ) has a defined verification\\nmethod and pass condition, or an explicit unresolved question. Each proposed\\ncheck identifies how to perform it and where its evidence will be retained.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Prepare work orders (WO) linking the definitions, scope, planned work, and verification requirements](DRAFT_WORK_ORDERS.md#prepare-work-orders-wo-linking-the-definitions-scope-planned-work-and-verification-requirements) links these contracts to work orders (WO). The execution\\nand verification processes later perform the checks and assess the evidence.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"16a9ea7323450e244b26edd2d1ad757048124b627bcfffa157c8b173f57f363c\"}]}, {\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-procedure36-20261011\\\\claude-311\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\ARTIFACT_AUTHORING.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"71de2cc92a470e2da25d525e3ad002b77faedb6ef0e69e2fa476a2fa4cb78ff5\", \"bytes\": 11691, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"intent\", \"title\": \"intent\", \"anchor\": \"intent\", \"start_line\": 76, \"end_line\": 84, \"content\": \"## intent\\n\\n### Checklist\\n\\n- Explain the present problem, who it affects, and the outcome wanted.\\n- What is the smallest useful outcome, and which needs and constraints are actually agreed?\\n- Say how the owner will know the change helped; an honest baseline may be `not measured`.\\n- State the important scope limits. Create a new intent when the desired outcome changes.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"291e8274f0c400a3d2d1ecdd56f2c10d68ab6cb40f667076a034e44d87c05e42\"}, {\"heading\": \"design-simplicity\", \"title\": \"Design simplicity\", \"anchor\": \"design-simplicity\", \"start_line\": 21, \"end_line\": 58, \"content\": \"## Design simplicity\\n\\nFor unfinished work, `harnessctl validate-draft REPO --artifact ID --json`\\nreports whether one supported draft has an admissible shape. Only exact values\\nat the same type and field in this evaluator's canonical template, and missing\\nor empty required authoring links, can be reported as incomplete. Invalid\\nidentity, dates, metadata shapes, actual endpoints, lifecycle history and\\ndisposition are errors. A missing non-template ID is an error. Background\\nfindings remain visible; this result makes no whole-repository validity claim.\\nComplete the authoring checklist and pass ordinary approval gates before\\nrequesting approval. Draft admission grants no authority to write or approve.\\n\\nChoose the least complex design that satisfies the agreed requirements and\\nconstraints. Justify material added complexity by the need it serves and why\\na simpler approach is insufficient. Consider the effort to understand, build,\\ntest, operate and change the whole solution. Defer speculative needs. Review\\nboth the necessity of the specified behavior and the simplicity of its\\nimplementation. Preserve correctness and the project's required quality and\\nsafety properties.\\n\\nStart from the smallest complete solution. Each project supplies its agreed\\nusers, scale, operating conditions and quality constraints. Agreed future needs\\ncan matter; imagined flexibility alone is insufficient. Credible risk analysis\\ncan justify prevention before an incident. Fewer lines, shorter specifications\\nor fewer tests do not establish simplicity; moving work to users, operations\\nor dependencies also has a cost.\\n\\nApply the relevant questions below before definition approval and during design\\nand implementation review. Return unjustified obligations or design complexity\\nfor revision through the existing decision process. Explain material choices\\nin the existing specification, design or review: the need, why a simpler option\\nis insufficient and the added cost. Scale the explanation to the decision; a\\nsmall choice may need one sentence, a significant design may use its existing\\narchitecture decision. Trivial changes need no separate comparison document.\\n\\nThese are human review standards. They introduce no separate KISS artifact,\\nmandatory score, receipt, automated simplicity gate or additional approval.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"29e9b6296f39215c4b2ad36659c0674f0b20650682db9aa1187fc97871cfdb06\"}, {\"heading\": \"verification\", \"title\": \"verification\", \"anchor\": \"verification\", \"start_line\": 126, \"end_line\": 135, \"content\": \"## verification\\n\\n### Checklist\\n\\n- Independence: expected values derive from the requirements and specifications, never from candidate output.\\n- A requirement-to-evidence matrix with a pass condition per requirement.\\n- Cover normal behavior and meaningful failure outcomes or risks; do not invent failure scenarios to fill a template.\\n- What accepted behavior or quality does each test or CI check establish? Cover distinct relevant outcomes, reuse applicable evidence, and avoid mirroring every implementation branch or combination.\\n- Pass criteria name the platforms and the evaluator.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"e6d36e5fe7a23845861e67079317f856b4f892d7f17c4f3d4dbdb4476edb7720\"}, {\"heading\": \"work_order\", \"title\": \"work_order\", \"anchor\": \"work_order\", \"start_line\": 136, \"end_line\": 146, \"content\": \"## work_order\\n\\n### Checklist\\n\\n- `[execution_scope].paths` is exact: files or component-prefix directories, nothing wider.\\n- `[assurance]` is classified and its rationale names what later decisions depend on.\\n- In scope and out of scope are both stated; the decision envelope says what the implementer may and may not decide.\\n- Stop conditions and the completion report format are stated.\\n- Is every planned change needed for the agreed outcome? Include removal or adjustment of obsolete behavior, tests and instructions within the approved scope.\\n- Inspect supporting paths before approval and assess the supplied plan through [Draft work orders](harness/DRAFT_WORK_ORDERS.md#prepare-work-orders-wo-linking-the-definitions-scope-planned-work-and-verification-requirements). Keep reasons in Expected change surface; do not add a separate planning artifact.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"e8d3be9bfae6d0679a08544ac587fa73e1a26e026d23373c29e1927e0803e7b8\"}]}]}",
  "is_error": false
}

  "content": "{\"resources\": [{\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/ARTIFACTS.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-procedure36-20261011\\\\claude-311\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\ARTIFACTS.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"2fa84648afedc03f3b811cb0e61d77ee1a1d52421ba3b3fa3eb435c4abd6eb89\", \"bytes\": 9622, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"artifact-types\", \"title\": \"Artifact types\", \"anchor\": \"artifact-types\", \"start_line\": 15, \"end_line\": 62, \"content\": \"## Artifact types\\n\\n\\u201cRequired\\u201d means the selected work needs coverage of that type. It does not\\nmean every change must create a new file. Reuse an existing active artifact\\nwhen its accepted meaning and scope still cover the work. Create a conditional\\nartifact only when its condition applies.\\n\\nFor an existing selected work order (WO), get its current state and next\\naction with:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\\n```\\n\\nBefore implementation, check whether that work order (WO) and its linked\\ndefinitions are eligible to start:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl preflight REPO --work-order WO-ID --phase start --json\\n```\\n\\nThe first command reports lifecycle context without evaluating checkpoint\\ngates. The second evaluates start readiness, including the states and coverage\\nof the selected definitions. Neither command approves or starts work.\\nHere, `REPO` is the absolute repository path and `WO-ID` is the actual ID of\\nthe selected work order (WO). Use the repository's selected released evaluator.\\n\\n| Type                  | ID prefix | Purpose                                                                                                                                                                      | When needed                                                                                                                                              |\\n|-----------------------|-----------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|\\n| `intent`              | `INT-`    | States the problem, intended outcome, and limits of responsibility.                                                                                                          | The work needs an accepted purpose. Reuse an intent (INT) when that purpose still applies.                                                               |\\n| `capability`          | `CAP-`    | Describes an ability that a human or agent should have.                                                                                                                      | Requirements (REQ) need a link to the capability (CAP) they support.                                                                                     |\\n| `requirement`         | `REQ-`    | States required behavior or a constraint, with a condition for acceptance.                                                                                                   | The change creates or changes an obligation. Reuse a requirement (REQ) only if its meaning stays unchanged.                                              |\\n| `specification`       | `SPEC-`   | Defines the detailed behavior, interfaces, constraints, and rejection conditions.                                                                                            | Each requirement (REQ) selected for implementation needs specification (SPEC) coverage.                                                                  |\\n| `architecture`        | `ARCH-`   | Defines system structure and boundaries needed to meet significant requirements (REQ).                                                                                       | An active architecture (ARCH) directly addresses a requirement (REQ) selected by the work order (WO).                                                    |\\n| `adr`                 | `ADR-`    | Records an architecture decision (ADR), the alternatives, and their consequences.                                                                                            | An applicable architecture (ARCH) declares that a significant decision is required.                                                                      |\\n| `verification`        | `VER-`    | Defines how requirements (REQ) will be checked and what counts as a pass.                                                                                                    | Each selected requirement (REQ) needs verification coverage. This is the verification contract (VER), not a test result.                                 |\\n| `work_order`          | `WO-`     | Defines the bounded work to execute and its governing artifacts. Approval grants work authority.                                                                             | Before execution. One work order (WO) may cover several requirements (REQ). Completed scope does not authorize later work.                               |\\n| `verification_record` | `VREC-`   | Binds work orders (WO), verification contracts (VER), evidence, and one exact candidate commit for assessment.                                                               | When verification tied to a commit is required. One record may cover several work orders (WO) at the same commit.                                        |\\n| `release_contract`    | `REL-`    | Defines permitted release scope, gates, rollback conditions, and authority limits.                                                                                           | When a release record (RLS) is proposed.                                                                                                                 |\\n| `release_record`      | `RLS-`    | Records preparation and the later release decision for verified work at one exact commit.                                                                                    | When a formal release is proposed. Preparing the record does not release the work.                                                                       |\\n| `decision`            | `DEC-`    | Records a question, options, recommendation, decision maker, affected artifacts, and the eventual answer. It can also record a deviation from one specification (SPEC) rule. | A question blocks a transition, affects more than one artifact, or must remain recorded after approval; or work cannot meet a specification (SPEC) rule. |\\n| `risk`                | `RISK-`   | Records a threat, an owner, and a next action. Scoring and categories are optional.                                                                                          | A concern needs follow-up. A separate decision (DEC) is used when the concern must block work.                                                           |\\n| `operating_contract`  | `OPS-`    | Defines continuing service, support, monitoring, or operational assurance obligations.                                                                                       | Ongoing operational commitments apply. Omitting the record does not prove those commitments have been met.                                               |\\n\\nCode, tests, acceptance scenarios, evidence files, commits, dashboards, tickets,\\nand conversations are not additional formal artifact types. They may support\\nor be referenced by formal artifacts but do not grant authority by themselves.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"47268c9ec1e3551fb547ad3d1ef7b8da0b773b52b3a4364f42c36cc64f98bf83\"}, {\"heading\": \"artifact-locations\", \"title\": \"Artifact locations\", \"anchor\": \"artifact-locations\", \"start_line\": 63, \"end_line\": 96, \"content\": \"## Artifact locations\\n\\nNew formal artifacts use these directories below\\n`docs/engineering/DOMAIN/`. `DOMAIN` is the selected domain name. These paths\\ndescribe storage, not authority; the artifact ID, metadata, and typed links\\nremain authoritative. Use the supported authoring command to obtain the actual\\npath. Do not move existing records merely to match this table.\\n\\n| Artifact | TYPE | Directory |\\n| --- | --- | --- |\\n| Intent (INT) | `intent` | `intent/` |\\n| Capability (CAP) | `capability` | `capabilities/` |\\n| Requirement (REQ) | `requirement` | `requirements/` |\\n| Specification (SPEC) | `specification` | `specifications/` |\\n| Architecture (ARCH) | `architecture` | `architecture/` |\\n| Architecture decision (ADR) | `adr` | `architecture/adr/` |\\n| Verification contract (VER) | `verification` | `verification/` |\\n| Work order (WO) | `work_order` | `work-orders/` |\\n| Verification record (VREC) | `verification_record` | `verification-records/` |\\n| Release contract (REL) | `release_contract` | `release/` |\\n| Release record (RLS) | `release_record` | `releases/` |\\n| Operating contract (OPS) | `operating_contract` | `operations/` |\\n| Decision (DEC) | `decision` | `decisions/` |\\n| Risk (RISK) | `risk` | `risks/` |\\n\\nThe same domain has `evidence/` for retained work-order evidence and\\n`acceptance/` for acceptance scenarios. These are supporting files, not new\\nformal artifact types. Legacy flat artifact locations remain readable and\\nMUST NOT be moved by an upgrade without separate authority.\\n\\nTemplates under `docs/engineering/templates/` are authoring assets. They are\\nexcluded from active-artifact validation and grant no authority. Use\\n`capture-verification` and `prepare-release` for commit-bound VREC and RLS\\ninstances; an edited template is not a substitute for their derived evidence.\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"8df276d2f837c4dcc663dbdb42c67cbb6f8fe3bf4abcb53acf86bc7538269a72\"}]}, {\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/DEFINITION_LINKS.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-procedure36-20261011\\\\claude-311\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\DEFINITION_LINKS.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"75b0c06f90fbb219fc31684a1a2d97987215dbada20e335656e45a57532c3010\", \"bytes\": 3505, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"links-between-definitions\", \"title\": \"Links between definitions\", \"anchor\": \"links-between-definitions\", \"start_line\": 3, \"end_line\": 35, \"content\": \"## Links between definitions\\n\\nAn arrow points from the artifact that records the link to the artifact it\\nreferences. These links describe meaning and coverage, not execution order.\\n\\nRepository validation, draft validation and work-order preflight share the\\ndeclared source/relation/target types.     "content": "+++\nid = \"INT-P3-001\"\ntype = \"intent\"\ntitle = \"Verify the rehearsal greeting output\"\nstatus = \"draft\"\nowners = [\"test-owner\"]\ncreated = \"2026-10-11\"\nupdated = \"2026-10-11\"\noutcome = \"The test operator can prove that greeting() returns exactly Hello rehearsal.\"\n\n[relations]\n+++\n\n# Verify the rehearsal greeting output\n\nSynthetic rehearsal input. This is not a real human decision.\n\n## In plain words\n\nAn operator needs to inspect the existing fixture greeting function and confirm its exact return value for a disposable lifecycle rehearsal.\n\n## Problem\n\nThe operator has no verified evidence that the fixture greeting function returns the expected string. Without a verification record, the rehearsal lifecycle cannot proceed.\n\n## Success measures\n\n| Measure | Today | When reached | Observed |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| Verified greeting output matches expected value | not measured | Exact match confirmed | Verification record in lifecycle-pilot evidence |\n\n## Not this\n\n- Real product changes or releases\n- Modification of the greeting source\n- External deployment or publication\n- Real human decisions or authority\n"
  }
}

This is not a real human decision.\n\n## In plain words\n\nThe greeting function must return the exact string `Hello rehearsal`.\n\n## Why\n\nThe lifecycle rehearsal requires a fixed, predictable greeting to verify the complete artifact chain from intent through release.\n\n## Behavior\n\n| Trigger | Response | On failure |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| Call greeting() | Returns the string `Hello rehearsal` | The verification check fails |\n\n## Examples\n\n### Normal\n\n**Given** the greeting module is available\n\n**When** greeting() is called\n\n**Then** the return value equals `Hello rehearsal`\n"

Commit the new content,\n119\tderived edges, selection changes and accepted receipt in that transaction. A\n120\tcompeting guard update or constraint conflict refuses; a process mutex is not\n121\tthe transaction boundary. Do not retry against newer input without caller action.\n122\t\n123\tImported content and repeated source identity cannot disagree. A new operation key\n126\tstill has its own guarded command/receipt; retrying the original key has no new\n127\teffect. A refusal creates no accepted receipt.\n128\t\n129\tDomain reads select either an immutable baseline or an explicit context/version.\n130\tExact revision, work context, comparison, impact, verification lineage and check\n131\tall return selection identity, provenance, evaluator identity and completeness.\n132\tComparison explicitly names both views. The pinned reference fixture has 12 governing IDs and 11 paths\n140\tfor WO-RLS-038; VREC dependencies remain separately classified.\n141\t\n142\t## Validation at each stage\n143\t\n144\t| Stage | Required assessment | Meaning of success |\n145\t| --- | --- | --- |\n146\t| Decode | Strict UTF-8, unique JSON keys, integer-only request numbers, closed wire shape, contained source paths and configured source identity | A request can be evaluated; no authority or mutation follows from decoding |\n147\t| Import | Complete Git manifest, raw byte/provenance equality, released parsing and relation validation, all referenced targets resolved | A faithful historical baseline; imported lifecycle claims acquire no new decision event |\n148\t| Draft creation/revision | Released standalone draft classification, server rights, protected-field comparison and exact input selection | A saved draft may retain the evaluator's narrow permitted incompleteness; malformed content or supplied invalid relations refuse |\n149\t| Freeze | Complete selection, resolvable declared edges, canonical manifest and version guards | An immutable baseline of that selection; draft members remain drafts |\n150\t| Context/read/check | Explicit view, exact released context/findings, original-path historical resolver and honest resource limits | A bounded observation; `complete` does not mean approved or gate-passing |\n151\t| Approval/verification/release | Unsupported in this sandbox | No remote command can confer a human decision or produce a verified/released record |\n152\t\n153\tThe draft stage uses the selected public 0.22.1 evaluator.   "content": "200\thas no meaningful narrower selection, repeat with a larger budget within the\n201\tfixed limits; if the minimum honest response cannot fit, refuse with 429.\n202\tUnresolved graph targets require a new complete view, not silent continuation at\n203\tan altered context version. No pagination cursor or server-side read session is\n204\tintroduced in this initial contract.\n205\t\n206\tThe baseline GET retrieves one canonical manifest resource, not a row query:\n207\t`{schema: \"se-harness-artifact-baseline/v1\", baseline_id, manifest}`. Validate the\n208\tmanifest with `BaselineManifest`, recompute its identity, and return the full\n209\tselection and resolved relations. The 2 MiB response limit still applies; reject\n210\tan import/freeze whose complete baseline response cannot fit before committing.\n211\tDo not clip the manifest to the graph-query row budget or label a fragment as B.\n212\tOperation GET returns exactly the stored accepted `remote-result/v1` object for\n213\tthe authenticated principal/project/key. Status GET reports project UUID, schema\n214\trevision, readiness, actual evaluator and component identities, and the supported\n215\tprotocols. Missing built/qualified identities keep readiness false. Neither GET\n216\trequires a context selector; operation lookup never guesses an acceptance.\n217\t\n218\t## Accepted receipts, refusals and retry order\n219\t\n220\t`server/contracts/result-v1.json` defines immutable accepted receipts and stable\n221\tremote refusal codes. Every successful mutation returns HTTP 200 and one UUIDv4\n222\t`receipt_id`. Store that entire result with the operation. Bind `request_digest`\n223\tto SHA-256 of `se-harness-remote-command/v1`, one LF, and canonical JSON of the\n224\tcomplete validated command. Canonicalize manifest entries as their declared set\n225\tbefore hashing; retain original document bytes and all expected versions. Reject\n226\tduplicate manifest entries before normalization. Credentials, connection metadata\n227\tand transport timestamps are outside the command and digest.\n228\t\n229\tAn accepted result's project `after` equals `before + 1`. For context open,\n230\tcontext `before` is null and `after` is zero. Create/revise increments the context\n231\tonce; freeze reports equal context versions. Import has a null context version\n232\trecord. The returned view names the imported/frozen baseline or resulting context;\n233\taffected revision IDs are sorted unique IDs created by this operation, so an\n234\tidentical-source reimport may report none. Receipt IDs are correlation identities,\n235\tnot substitutes for revision or baseline hashes.\n236\t`affected_artifacts` supplies the corresponding artifact/revision bindings, so\n237\tcreation with automatic ID allocation returns the allocated artifact ID directly.\n238\tIts revision set must equal `affected_revision_ids`; a refusal returns both empty.\n239\t\n240\tApply checks in this order:\n241\t\n242\t1. Authenticate and enforce project/operation access, then perform bounded strict\n243\t   decoding and shape checks. Reject unknown fields such as caller-supplied actor.\n244\t2. For a valid command, compute its request digest and inspect the accepted key.\n245\t   Equal digest returns the original result immediately, even after versions or\n246\t   the configured tuple change. Different digest returns key-reuse conflict.\n247\t   Current project access remains required; never evaluate a retry as a new write.\n248\t3. For a new key, check the supported tuple, known selections, then project,\n249\t   context and selected-revision guards in that order. Refuse the first mismatch.\n250\t4. Check source/protected input and invoke the released evaluator against the\n251\t   bound selection. Invalid draft/import, incomplete freeze and unavailable\n252\t   mandatory bindings have distinct 422 codes. Resource refusal is 429.\n253\t5. Recheck guards and selection in the write transaction, write all effects and\n254\t   receipt, then commit. On a concurrent equal-key conflict, read the winner's\n255\t   committed receipt. Never update a stale request's expectations automatically.\n256\t\n257\tMalformed input uses 400, unauthenticated 401, forbidden 403, unknown accessible\n258\tidentity 404, stale/key/source/tuple conflict 409, invalid content 422 and resource\n259\trefusal 429. The schema enumerates the `HAG_REMOTE_*` codes. These codes wrap the\n260\tremote operation; existing evaluator codes and JSON remain unchanged. Refusals\n261\thave no receipt and no affected revisions. Populate correlation/view/versions\n262\tonly when known and authorized; otherwise use null. When versions are returned\n263\ton refusal, before equals after. Read refusals use null command correlation and\n264\tversions. Denial must not leak another project's identities or findings.\n265\t\n266\tThe schemas describe the selected evaluator profile as well as wire shapes.\n267\tTheir pinned evaluator constants are compatibility checks: a well-formed but\n268\tdifferent version/archive/payload is 409, not malformed JSON. Keep those checks\n269\tseparate from primitive shape decoding and accepted-key recovery. Strictly\n270\tdecode base64 and require its canonical encoding before digest calculation;\n271\tinvalid base64 is malformed input, while invalid decoded TOML is invalid draft\n272\tcontent. A successful shape check alone never supplies an admission decision.\n273\t\n274\tA lost response, evaluator process crash or ambiguous database commit is not an\n275\taccepted or refused receipt. The client reports transport uncertainty and uses\n276\toperation lookup or an identical retry. A 404 lookup alone cannot prove that a\n277\tconcurrent transaction rolled back. A known precommit failure leaves no effects;\n278\tan unknown commit must be reconciled against the authoritative operation store.\n279\t\n280\t## Scenario and schema qualification\n281\t\n282\t`tests/hosted_artifact_graph/fixtures/scenarios-v1.json` maps all twelve\n283\tVER-HAG-001 cases to explicit positive/failure outcomes and storage support. Every\n284\thosted execution entry is `unperformed`. Its wire examples contain synthetic\n285\tidentities and demonstrate shape only; their apparent receipt values are not\n286\tobserved acceptances. The original fixed canonical vectors remain unchanged.\n287\t\n288\tFor independent shape qualification, install `jsonschema==4.26.0` in a disposable\n289\tenvironment and run its Python against:\n290\t\n291\t```text\n292\tpython -I -B tests/hosted_artifact_graph/validate_wire_contracts.py\n293\tpython -m unittest tests.test_hosted_artifact_graph\n294\t```\n295\t\n296\tThe first command registers the three local schemas without network resolution,\n297\tvalidates fixed vectors and the complete source manifest, and exercises accepted\n298\tand rejected shapes. It does not introduce a core runtime dependency. The second\n299\tcommand checks fixed bytes and digests through the existing test entry point.\n300\tNeither command executes service admission, transactions, Cypher or recovery.\n301\t\n302\t## Physical representation\n303\t\n304\tProject stores identity, schema revision and the integer command guard. Artifact\n305\tstores project-scoped identity. A preview never changes graph state. Actor strings\n15\tare synthetic test inputs, not authenticated human consent.\n16\t\n17\tThe digest binds project/context versions, all selected revision IDs, retained\n18\tsnapshot, exact command/file inputs, principal and released evaluator identity.\n19\tMode and operation key are excluded from the preview binding, but included in\n20\tthe accepted request digest used for principal-scoped retry lookup.\n21\t\n22\t## Permitted file effects\n23\t\n24\t| Action | Permitted effects in the disposable projection |\n25\t| --- | --- |\n26\t| check / preflight / validate | No file writes |\n27\t| transition | Only selected newly authored artifacts and evaluator sidecars declared by their records |\n28\t| decide | Selected new decision and its explicitly linked rehearsal risk |\n29\t| raise-risk | New risk and optional paired decision at released domain paths |\n30\t| handoff | Declared bounded file inputs within the selected work order's scope; released handoff packet and evaluator evidence |\n31\t| capture-verification | New VREC and its released evaluator sidecar |\n32\t| prepare-release | New RLS and its released evaluator sidecar |\n33\t\n34\tHandoff first uses the released procedure's fixed `evidence` preparation command,\n35\tthen `check --checkpoint handoff --from-git`. It exposes no arbitrary evidence\n36\tcommand. File inputs cannot alter formal records, harness selection/instructions,\n37\tGit configuration, hooks, or executables used by the service. The service never\n38\truns source tests. The local qualification runner supplies observed test evidence.\n39\t\n40\tThe adapter enumerates every regular file before and after the operation. It\n41\trejects deletions, links, unexpected paths, missing sidecars, imported-record\n42\tchanges and evaluator failure before opening an acceptance transaction.\n43\t\n44\t## Snapshots and Git\n45\t\n46\tEach accepted operation retains an immutable complete snapshot: exact file bytes,\n47\tself-contained Git history and its source/test-candidate identities. Content\n48\tdigests bind the snapshot to the resulting baseline. Context heads, artifact\n49\trevisions, snapshot and receipt share one database transaction. Temporary Git\n50\trepositories are discarded after the result is accepted or refused.\n51\t\n52\tCapture first commits the exact input projection as test candidate P. P excludes\n53\tthe new VREC. The output retains the input hosted baseline B, source fixture S,\n54\tP and evaluator identity. Actual implementation candidate C is recorded separately\n55\tby qualification. Subsequent commits never alter the candidate stored in a VREC.\n56\t\n57\tExport selects an immutable baseline, checks its complete snapshot and Git bundle,\n58\tand returns exact bounded bytes. The installed client writes only to a new explicit\n59\tdestination and leaves an incomplete marker until every file hash is checked.\n60\tNo export writes to or synchronizes an authoritative repository.\n61\t\n62\t## Bounds and failure categories\n63\t\n64\tKeep 4 MiB requests, 2 MiB responses, 1 MiB individual file inputs, two concurrent\n65\tprojections, 120-second evaluator calls and 10,000 retained artifact revisions.\n66\tA pilot snapshot/export that exceeds the response bound is refused atomically.\n67\tRetained complete snapshots have a separate explicit project count bound of 128.\n68\tThis is a small private pilot, not an unbounded document store.\n69\t\n70\tBoundary/malformed requests, stale inputs, evaluator refusals, invalid output and\n71\tunknown transport outcomes remain distinct. Read the operation receipt after a\n72\tlost reply. A changed request under the same accepted key is a conflict. The\n73\tservice does not automatically rerun a mutation at a newer version.\n74\t\n75\t## Bounded Cypher reads\n76\t\n77\tThe existing `cypher` MCP/HTTP read selects one exact view. Its request uses the\n78\tread schema, not the lifecycle command schema. Read the public labels and\n79\tproperties in [the physical representation](../../docs/notes/hosted-artifact-graph.md#physical-representation)\n80\tbefore selecting a query. `Artifact` and `Revision` are distinct public labels.\n81\t\n82\tThe admitted form has one `MATCH` or `OPTIONAL MATCH` path, labelled nodes,\n83\toptional parameterized property comparisons, `RETURN`, and a numeric `LIMIT`.\n84\t\n85\tRelationship paths are supported. Use `-[edge:TYPE]->` or `<-[edge:TYPE]-` between\n86\tlabelled nodes, with a public relationship type from the physical representation.\n87\tEach node and relationship needs a distinct variable name. Anonymous relationship\n88\tsyntax such as `-[:TYPE]->` is refused; a refusal of that form does not mean\n89\trelationship traversal is unsupported. Return-column aliases (`AS alias`) are\n90\toptional.\n91\t\n92\tFor example, with `parameters` containing a selected artifact ID under `artifact`:\n93\t\n94\t```cypher\n95\tMATCH (r:Revision) WHERE r.artifact_id = $artifact RETURN r.artifact_id AS id, r.status AS status LIMIT 10\n96\t```\n97\t\n98\tUse the response's completeness fields with the requested row/byte/depth budget.\n99\tThis example supplies no project filter: the service binds the selected project\n100\tand view. Unlabelled nodes, `WITH`, aggregation and arbitrary database syntax are\n101\tnot part of this small public read form. A rejected query has not completed a read.\n102\tKeep the exact refusal; consult the documented form before constructing another\n103\trequest. Do not weaken query admission or bypass it through a direct database call.\n104\t"

Before review, read [AUTHORITY.md#decision-rights](AUTHORITY.md#decision-rights) and [AUTHORITY.md#delegation-and-separation](AUTHORITY.md#delegation-and-separation). Before approving work, read [WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#assurance-classification](WORK_AND_EVIDENCE.md#assurance-classification). For an open or deferred decision, read [RISKS_AND_DECISIONS.md#blocking-decisions](RISKS_AND_DECISIONS.md#blocking-decisions).\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"9aa32693606102a5aa74b2e4a614aed6904471e5f618dea93356baac97c86ca2\"}, {\"heading\": \"apply-the-selected-transitions\", \"title\": \"Apply the selected transitions\", \"anchor\": \"apply-the-selected-transitions\", \"start_line\": 221, \"end_line\": 270, \"content\": \"### Apply the selected transitions\\n\\n**Inputs:** The exact artifact IDs, target states, accountable human identities,\\nreviewed file digests, decisions from [Obtain the human's decision](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#obtain-the-humans-decision), and clarification answers from\\n[Define the change](DEFINE_CHANGE.md#procedure) that must be retained in the transition reason.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** Only explicitly selected definitions and work orders\\n  (WO) receive the applied state and lifecycle-history entries.\\n- **Transient working material:** The preview and apply results, including\\n  any refused transition.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Confirm that the reviewed artifact contents still match the human decision.\\n2. Select only the named transitions. Approve governing definitions before\\n   dependent work, or use one explicitly selected packet for mutually\\n   dependent definitions when the evaluator permits it.\\n3. Preview the transition command below.\\n4. Compare every selected ID, target state, actor, and reported effect with\\n   the human decision. Resolve failed gates before proceeding.\\n5. Recheck reviewed definition digests immediately before applying.\\n6. Repeat the passing command with `--apply`. Do not add related artifacts\\n   that the human did not select.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set ARTIFACT-ID=TARGET --decision ARTIFACT-ID=ACTOR --json\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set ARTIFACT-ID=TARGET --decision ARTIFACT-ID=ACTOR --apply --json\\n```\\n\\nFor each clarification below the formal-decision threshold, add\\n`--reason \\\"ARTIFACT-ID=Question and confirmed answer\\\"` to both the preview\\nand apply command. Use the actual question and answer. When one artifact\\nhas several such answers, combine them into one reason value for that ID.\\nKeep each complete `ID=TEXT` value as one argument. Confirm the saved reason\\nin the resulting lifecycle entry before discarding the working notes.\\n\\nFor approval, `TARGET` is `approved`. For rejection, use `rejected` and add\\n`--reason \\\"ARTIFACT-ID=Rejection reason\\\"` to both commands. Rejection is\\nterminal. For a packet, repeat `--set` and `--decision` for every explicitly\\nselected artifact. Preview and apply must contain the same assignments.\\n\\n**Completion:** The evaluator reports the actual applied transitions, or the\\nspecific refusal. A successful preview alone changes nothing.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Confirm the authorization result](AUTHORIZE_WORK.md#confirm-the-authorization-result) confirms the resulting authority and readiness.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"c5215dec9f35d1a99bf6e4d658a594a31dc544131e70db49b1b8f79514e124b8\"}]}, {\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/AUTHORITY.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-procedure36-20261011\\\\claude-311\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\AUTHORITY.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"da7202bf8d1beee631e9b4b5ba01c04b440fbf76d200ccbccbb8781a59f88431\", \"bytes\": 16163, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"decision-rights\", \"title\": \"Decision rights\", \"anchor\": \"decision-rights\", \"start_line\": 19, \"end_line\": 80, \"content\": \"## Decision rights\\n\\nA profile describes who performs an action. A decision right describes which\\naction that person or agent is authorized to perform. Being a Human does not\\ngrant every right in this table. Being an Agent grants none by itself.\\n\\nUse the artifact's recorded ownership and any existing explicit delegation to\\nidentify the accountable human. Resolve a team name or ownership label to the\\nhuman making this decision. A proposed `owners` value, an example actor name,\\nor authorship of a draft MUST NOT assign decision authority.\\n\\nApproval, verification acceptance, and release decisions MUST be made by an\\nauthorized human. An agent MAY prepare the material and apply that human's\\nrecorded decision when authorized to do so. Applying the decision does not\\nmake the agent its author. The selected IDs, reviewed content, target states,\\nand reason MUST still match the decision at apply time.\\n\\nThe following stable IDs name rights and operations, not additional profiles.\\nAll applicable gates remain required.\\n\\nThese DR-* identifiers come from the selected evaluator's decision-right\\ncatalogue. Use the right and exact action returned by that evaluator. Legacy\\nowner labels identify recorded accountabilities; they do not create additional\\nHuman/Agent profiles or assign a decision to a person by themselves.\\n\\n| Right ID                   | Accountable profile and authority                                                                                                  | Required input                                                                                                    | Result                                                                                               |\\n|----------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|\\n| `DR-DEFINITION-DECIDE`     | Human authorized to decide the selected definition.                                                                                | Complete definition, exact proposed state, and required checks.                                                   | Decision on that definition only.                                                                    |\\n| `DR-WO-SELECT`             | Human authorized to approve the work scope.                                                                                        | Complete governing chain, bounded work order, and assurance classification.                                       | Approval or rejection of the selected work order.                                                    |\\n| `DR-WO-START`              | Human or Agent executing the selected work under its recorded approval.                                                            | Unchanged approved scope and passing start checks.                                                                | The selected work order becomes `in_progress`.                                                       |\\n| `DR-WO-COMPLETE`           | Human or Agent executing the selected work under its recorded approval.                                                            | Completed scope, passing handoff checks, and retained evidence.                                                   | The selected work order becomes `implemented`.                                                       |\\n| `DR-VREC-PREPARE`          | Human or Agent under the approval of every selected work order.                                                                    | Exact candidate, selected work and verification contracts, retained evidence, and passing preparation checks.     | One `ready` verification record; no acceptance decision.                                             |\\n| `DR-VREC-DECIDE`           | Human authorized to accept verification for the selected candidate.                                                                | Ready record, assessed evidence, and the exact verify, reject, or supersede decision.                             | Decision on that verification record only.                                                           |\\n| `DR-DELIVERY-SELECT`       | Human authorized for the chosen integration or release-preparation path.                                                           | Exact candidate, destination, and coverage required by that path.                                                 | One path-specific instruction.                                                                       |\\n| `DR-RLS-PREPARE`           | Human authorizes the release inputs; a Human or Agent may carry out the preparation.                                               | Exact release contract, candidate, included work, verification coverage, version, and passing preparation checks. | One `ready` release record; no release decision.                                                     |\\n| `DR-RLS-DECIDE`            | Human authorized to make the release decision.                                                                                     | Ready release record, assessed contract obligations, and the exact release or reject decision.                    | Decision on that release record only.                                                                |\\n| `DR-EXTERNAL-ACTION`       | Human authorized for the exact external action. A Human or Agent may execute the authorized operation.                             | Action, immutable target, destination, prerequisites, and recovery conditions.                                    | Authorization limited to that operation; the subsequent tool result records its actual effects.      |\\n| `DR-RELATED-RECORD-SELECT` | Human or Agent; no decision authority is needed for inspection.                                                                    | Exact existing artifact ID.                                                                                       | Read-only selection.                                                                                 |\\n| `DR-REMEDIATION-SCOPE`     | Human authorized to change the affected definition or work scope.                                                                  | Failed criterion and proposed bounded correction.                                                                 | Explicit authority for the revised scope.                                                            |\\n| `DR-DECISION-DISPOSE`      | Human accountable for the artifact blocked by the decision; for a deviation, the human accountable for the affected specification. | Open or deferred decision, selected option or disposition, exact reason, and any required revisit trigger.        | Recorded disposition and any declared paired-risk effect. Other blocked artifacts keep their states. |\\n\\nFor a lifecycle decision, the `--decision` value identifies its actual\\ndecision-maker. When an agent applies a human decision, that value is the\\nhuman who made it. For start and completion under approved execution, it is\\nthe actual Human or Agent executor. Supplying either identity does not grant\\nauthority. Preparation `--owner` values identify the preparation actor, not\\na future assurance or release decision-maker.\\n\\nFor `decide`, when an owner label differs from the actual human's identity,\\nuse `--authority-owner OWNER` only after establishing that human's authority\\nfor the exact decision. Keep `--decision ACTOR` as the actual human. The\\nevaluator checks that OWNER holds the existing decision right and records it\\nseparately as `authority_owner`. With the option absent, ACTOR must itself\\nmatch an eligible owner. The command does not authenticate either claim or\\ngrant consent. Do not infer a binding from the decision's own owners, an\\nearlier approval, Git configuration or the agent's identity.\\n\\nSilence, elapsed time, a commit, a pull request, tool execution, or a passing\\ncheck MUST NOT count as a human decision. A rejection MUST include a non-empty\\nreason. Supersession MUST name exactly one eligible successor. The decision\\nand its resulting state MUST remain specific to the selected artifact.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"6ecaeb29429bcaa4eac99857f2f921e270c4865d1edeec96f536d09eb6c3130a\"}]}, {\"resource\": \"docs/engineering/harness/EXECUTE_WORK.md\", \"path\": \"C:\\\\Users\\\\mathi\\\\Documents\\\\Codex\\\\2026-09-20\\\\verity-plane-plugin-verity-plane-se\\\\work\\\\hag-procedure36-20261011\\\\claude-311\\\\native\\\\inputs\\\\released-resources\\\\docs\\\\engineering\\\\harness\\\\EXECUTE_WORK.md\", \"release\": {\"archive_sha256\": \"cb35c3c4eb51fe0fca575a9340b1cba6cd63b5f7133a0ebeb3fd32b4ed863053\", \"payload_sha256\": \"0e7b7c2bc10b06150c6c1ebe88d796d047f5d906bdfb5ef4e00914a56c79e7ff\", \"version\": \"0.22.1\"}, \"sha256\": \"f094e7bc796ba1002c3fb2848adaccce6c4887e3d92503911bcafa4504a644fe\", \"bytes\": 11979, \"sections\": [{\"heading\": \"read-this-when\", \"title\": \"Read this when\", \"anchor\": \"read-this-when\", \"start_line\": 3, \"end_line\": 6, \"entry_condition\": true, \"content\": \"## Read this when\\n\\nThe evaluator selects work start, implementation or completion under matching authority.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"b7fc3711b437c73d3cc5cce46c0a8bb7f1bc3c8c98ba650b4b046950d69a7652\"}, {\"heading\": \"before-this-action\", \"title\": \"Before this action\", \"anchor\": \"before-this-action\", \"start_line\": 7, \"end_line\": 10, \"entry_condition\": true, \"content\": \"## Before this action\\n\\nRead the selected work order and governing artifacts returned by the evaluator. Before using its execution grant, read [AUTHORITY.md#authority-from-work-approval](AUTHORITY.md#authority-from-work-approval). For a scope finding, read [RESULTS.md#if-a-procedure-is-blocked](RESULTS.md#if-a-procedure-is-blocked).\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"5581ca4e192b2a3847952cdb19582916ed96242a14941b1e5a8a610be996e1dd\"}, {\"heading\": \"start-the-selected-work\", \"title\": \"Start the selected work\", \"anchor\": \"start-the-selected-work\", \"start_line\": 70, \"end_line\": 104, \"content\": \"### Start the selected work\\n\\n**Inputs:** The approved work order (WO), actual executor identity, and\\ncurrent execution context.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** The selected work order (WO) becomes `in_progress`\\n  with a lifecycle-history entry when start succeeds.\\n- **Transient working material:** Start-check and transition results.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. If the work order (WO) is already `in_progress`, inspect the recorded start\\n   and resume unapplied work. Do not replay the start transition.\\n2. Otherwise, run start preflight under the existing approval.\\n3. Preview the transition to `in_progress` using the actual executor identity.\\n4. Apply the same transition only when preflight and the preview pass.\\n5. Inspect the resulting state.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl preflight REPO --work-order WO-ID --phase start --json\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set WO-ID=in_progress --decision WO-ID=ACTOR --json\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set WO-ID=in_progress --decision WO-ID=ACTOR --apply --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\\n```\\n\\n**Completion:** The selected work order (WO) is `in_progress`, or its start\\nis blocked with an explicit reason. Existing approval covers a permitted\\nstart; no duplicate human start decision is required.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Implement the approved scope](EXECUTE_WORK.md#implement-the-approved-scope) performs the authorized implementation.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"2275126fa8b418eccbaacb1972d0140709337470ba8d190b6566fd16854328d1\"}, {\"heading\": \"retain-the-implementation-evidence\", \"title\": \"Retain the implementation evidence\", \"anchor\": \"retain-the-implementation-evidence\", \"start_line\": 155, \"end_line\": 210, \"content\": \"### Retain the implementation evidence\\n\\n**Inputs:** The implemented changes, verification contracts (VER), required\\nrepository checks, and work-order evidence locations.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Retained evidence files:** Actual check results, review findings, their\\n  resolution, and Git-derived handoff evidence at authorized evidence paths.\\n- **Formal artifacts:** Evidence references may be added where the work order\\n  (WO) requires them. No lifecycle state changes here.\\n- **Transient working material:** A summary of remaining evidence gaps.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. Run every required check using its specified inputs and environment.\\n2. Retain the command arguments, working directory, runtime identity, exit\\n   status, output, and relevant file paths or digests.\\n3. Preserve failed results alongside later corrections and successful reruns.\\n4. Retain the material diff-review findings and their resolution.\\n5. Confirm that all required evidence files exist at the locations named by\\n   the work order (WO) or verification contract (VER).\\n6. Run review preflight.\\n7. Run the Git-derived handoff check with the trusted `BASE` from [Establish the execution context](EXECUTE_WORK.md#establish-the-execution-context).\\n8. Inspect its written evidence and reported findings. Correct in-scope gaps\\n   and rerun affected checks.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl preflight REPO --work-order WO-ID --phase review --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --checkpoint handoff --from-git BASE --json\\n```\\n\\nFor ordinary files listed in the work order\\u2019s `evidence_paths`, confirm that\\neach file exists inside the repository and contains the required evidence.\\nThese files do not need a machine header. Use the supported evidence command\\nfor generated packets; do not edit their evaluator-owned binding fields.\\n\\nThe Git-derived handoff command writes retained evidence. Confirm its output\\npaths are covered by the approved preparation scope. If the evaluator calls\\nfor creating or rebinding the handoff evidence packet, use:\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl evidence REPO --artifact WO-ID --checkpoint handoff --json\\n```\\n\\nThis command also writes a file. It does not run tests or make an unsupported\\nclaim true. Rerun the required handoff check after correcting the evidence gap.\\n\\n**Completion:** Required implementation checks pass and the actual evidence\\nis retained, or the exact missing or failed checks remain visible.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Record implementation completion](EXECUTE_WORK.md#record-implementation-completion) requires passing handoff results. Verification later\\nassesses these retained files; they are not disposable planning notes.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"4cccfe03ac5fe219da997051bb91053576f18e67e834f0036f93e9856e8f12a2\"}]}]}",

For a scope finding, read [RESULTS.md#if-a-procedure-is-blocked](RESULTS.md#if-a-procedure-is-blocked).\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"5581ca4e192b2a3847952cdb19582916ed96242a14941b1e5a8a610be996e1dd\"}, {\"heading\": \"start-the-selected-work\", \"title\": \"Start the selected work\", \"anchor\": \"start-the-selected-work\", \"start_line\": 70, \"end_line\": 104, \"content\": \"### Start the selected work\\n\\n**Inputs:** The approved work order (WO), actual executor identity, and\\ncurrent execution context.\\n\\n**Output:**\\n\\n- **Formal artifacts:** The selected work order (WO) becomes `in_progress`\\n  with a lifecycle-history entry when start succeeds.\\n- **Transient working material:** Start-check and transition results.\\n\\n**Actions:**\\n\\n1. If the work order (WO) is already `in_progress`, inspect the recorded start\\n   and resume unapplied work. Do not replay the start transition.\\n2. Otherwise, run start preflight under the existing approval.\\n3. Preview the transition to `in_progress` using the actual executor identity.\\n4. Apply the same transition only when preflight and the preview pass.\\n5. Inspect the resulting state.\\n\\n**Harness commands:**\\n\\n```text\\nharnessctl preflight REPO --work-order WO-ID --phase start --json\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set WO-ID=in_progress --decision WO-ID=ACTOR --json\\nharnessctl transition REPO --set WO-ID=in_progress --decision WO-ID=ACTOR --apply --json\\nharnessctl check REPO --artifact WO-ID --json\\n```\\n\\n**Completion:** The selected work order (WO) is `in_progress`, or its start\\nis blocked with an explicit reason. Existing approval covers a permitted\\nstart; no duplicate human start decision is required.\\n\\n**Later use:** [Implement the approved scope](EXECUTE_WORK.md#implement-the-approved-scope) performs the authorized implementation.\\n\\n\", \"content_sha256\": \"2275126fa8b418eccbaacb1972d0140709337470ba8d190b6566fd16854328d1\"}]}]}",
